### 🤖 ASSISTANT — 2026-09-28T18:54:43.744Z

All four agents signed `docs/design-consensus.md` AGREE, three without reservation and product with one non-blocking note, and nothing is left open. I checked it and the evidence holds: all nine scripts behind it re-run to exactly the output they saved, and every figure I spot-checked appears in that output. That includes account 14's Site B (−2.16), 46 of 341, 34 of 99, 0.289, 1,161, 110, 253,149, the [2,4,6,20] case and every golden row.

**What they agreed**
1. **Normality rule:** they adopted the statistician's R2\* rule unchanged. It uses a threshold of 2, a spread floor of 1 and at least 4 eligible weeks. The status is read from the same whole-number range the admin sees, so the two can't contradict each other: 0 disagreements in 7,136 location-weeks. A normally busy location dropping to zero is now caught 98% of the time (96% for calls); the current plan catches 78% (37%). Account 6's spike week still flags all 15 sites.
2. **Presentation:**
   - Labels: "▲ Higher than usual", "▼ Lower than usual", "Within usual range", "Not enough history yet (N of 4 weeks needed)".
   - Ranges read "Usually X–Y a week". No severity tier, and no z-score or median on screen.
   - Ranking: flagged locations first, then by how unusual they are, with "lower" ahead of "higher" on a tie.
3. **Contract gaps:**
   - `baseline` is always present with `weeksUsed`, plus one top-level `minimumEligibleWeeks: 4`.
   - `earliestWeek` is never null and is the week of the account's first event; a week before it returns 400.
   - The type filter never changes which locations appear.
   - A count exactly on the edge of the range is normal.
4. **Data layer:** every SQL Server finding is adopted as written.

**Where they changed their minds**
- The statistician withdrew its own claim that the two ways of computing the band's midpoint differ by less than 0.01. The real gap is up to 0.289 and changes one status. The decision stood, and a test case now pins it.
- The architect switched to the statistician's rule that flagged locations always rank above normal ones.
- The architect and product crossed positions on `earliestWeek` for the account with no events, then settled on "never null".

**Decisions for you**
1. **Approve the design**, so I can write it into `PLAN.md` §13 and replace the text of §5.1–§5.3 and the §7 golden values.
2. **Default account changes from 12 to 14.** Under the new rule, account 12 has nothing flagged in the default week. Account 14 is the only account in the seed with a flagged location that week: Site B, 2 against a usual 3–12, "Lower than usual". The flag is only just over the line (z −2.16), so the README will say that about 4% of location-weeks are flagged by design.
3. **Starter files move into `db/`.** `seed.sql` and `schema.sql` are at the repo root, not in `db/` as `PLAN.md` and `CLAUDE.md` say. The agents propose a content-unchanged `git mv` in Phase 0. Since `CLAUDE.md` says those files are never modified, say whether a move is acceptable or you'd rather fix the paths in the docs.
4. **Limits to accept for the README:**
   - A location that usually gets 2 or fewer events a week can never show "lower".
   - A location halving its usual 6 or more a week is caught in only 34 of 99 real cases.
   - A spike widens later ranges for up to 7 weeks.
   - On burstier real data, false flags could rise to about 5–8% per side.

`PLAN.md` is unchanged. I'll write the §13 entry once you approve.

The four agents are idle but still running. Once you've decided, I'll log the debate and your decisions in `AI_LOG.md` (entry 18), run the export and commit. Tell me if you want them stopped now.

```
5. `centre = T(median)` — T of the raw median, **not** the median of the transformed values (they differ for even counts); `madT` = median of |T(cᵢ) − centre| over the eligible counts; `spread = max(1.4826 × madT, 1.0)`
   (1.4826 makes the MAD comparable to a standard deviation; 1.0 is the Poisson SD on this scale).
6. `lowT = centre − 2·spread`, `highT = centre + 2·spread`.
   `low = lowT ≤ T(0) ? 0 : ⌈(lowT/2)² − 0.375⌉` (the guard is mandatory: squaring a negative `lowT` would create a false lower edge);
   `high = ⌊(highT/2)² − 0.375⌋`.
7. Status from the integers only: `count < low` → `below`; `count > high` → `above`; otherwise `normal` (a count equal to `low` or `high` is `normal`).
   The displayed range "usually low–high" therefore can never contradict the status.
8. `deviation = (T(count) − centre) / spread`, full precision internally; rounded to 2 dp (away from zero) only in the API response.
9. Ranking of locations: `insufficient_data` last (among themselves by name); then flagged (`above`/`below`) before `normal`; then |deviation|
   descending on the unrounded value; then `below` before `above`; then location name ascending (ordinal).

Constants live in `NormalityOptions { BaselineWeeks = 8, MinimumEligibleWeeks = 4, BandWidth = 2.0, SpreadFloor = 1.0 }`.
Known limit: a series whose usual median is ≤ 2 can never be `below` (a drop to 0 is within normal variation there).
```

- **Rules for bad input:**
  - A malformed `week` value such as `2026-13-01` returns 400 (API-46).
  - Fixing a bad URL replaces the history entry instead of adding one, so Back doesn't return to the bad URL (UI-38).
  - When the account changes, the week falls back to the default if it's no longer valid, and the type is kept (UI-39).
  - An error state appears when the API can't be reached (UI-40).
- **Capitalisation of footnote lines:** the approved strings start lowercase ("compared with…", "inbound events…"). I kept them verbatim. You need to decide whether the frontend may capitalise the first letter.
- **Empty account (20):** I assumed the empty message replaces both the summary and the table. The consensus only says when the message appears, not what it replaces.
- **Summary wording under a type filter:** C-06 always says "inbound events", even when the filter is Calls. That is accurate but loose; "calls" would read better.

Where I think PLAN is wrong or incomplete:
1. PLAN §13 §5.4 has only the "at this location" method line. The "for this account" line for the summary comes from the consensus §9 sign-off note, not from PLAN text. §13 should add it.
2. The §5.4 route example still says `account=12`. §13 changes D5 to 14 but doesn't update that example.
3. "Not enough history yet (0 of 4 weeks needed)" is correct but awkward for the earliest weeks (account 14 on 02-02). It is acceptable, and changing it needs your approval.
4. The §13 golden row for account 14 lists "Sites C, A, D" without saying that is the ranking order. My query confirms the order B, C, A, D. Consider stating it explicitly in PLAN.

## Next
- **test-author:** assert only values tagged `GOLDEN` or `SPEC`. Ask the coordinator whether the Appendix A values (week after the spike, zero-activity location, account 14 early weeks, tie ordering, single-site) should be added to PLAN §13 as golden values. They cover required scenarios that the golden table currently doesn't.
- **frontend:** copy §0.1 exactly as written. Don't use §0.2 until it's approved.
- **Me, after each phase:** I'll run the curl and URL checks in §3–§4 and report PASS/FAIL per criterion id.</result>

## Deviations
- **`watch: false` for tests:** I made `ng test` run once by default so `npm test` always finishes. `npx ng test --watch` still gives watch mode.
- **Deleted `.vscode/` and `web/README.md`:** both were CLI boilerplate full of comments.

## Uncertain
- **Port 5000 conflict (needs a decision):** on this machine port 5000 is held by macOS AirPlay Receiver (`ControlCenter`, which answers `Server: AirTunes/950.7.1` with 403). If the backend binds to 5000, it will either fail to start or requests will go to AirPlay. I kept 5000 as instructed. Options: turn off AirPlay Receiver (System Settings → General → AirDrop &amp; Handoff), or pick another port such as 5080. That is a one-line change in `web/proxy.conf.json` plus the backend's launch settings, and the backend track needs to agree.
- **Zoneless:** I kept the CLI default (no `zone.js` and no `provideZoneChangeDetection`). This fits the signals/OnPush approach, but PLAN.md doesn't say either way.
- **No catch-all route:** there is no `**` route, so unknown paths show a blank page. The brief asked for a single route plus the empty-path redirect, so I didn't add one. Tell me if a `**` → `dashboard` redirect is wanted.
- **Eager loading:** `DashboardPage` is loaded directly rather than with `loadComponent`, since it is the only route.
- **Build budgets:** unchanged from the CLI defaults (component styles warn at 4 kB, error at 8 kB).

- **Test-author:** specs are picked up from `src/**/*.spec.ts` with Vitest globals (`tsconfig.spec.json`). The DOM environment is jsdom.
- **Coordinator:** a merge needs no root-level changes from this track. The port 5000 decision should be settled before Phase 3.</result>

All test projects have `OutputType=Exe` (xunit.v3 needs it), `IsPackable=false`, global usings for `Xunit` and `Shouldly`, and reference `Microsoft.NET.Test.Sdk`, `xunit.v3`, `xunit.runner.visualstudio` and `Shouldly`. **`Program` visibility check:** I added a temporary test calling `new WebApplicationFactory&lt;Program&gt;()` and fetching `/`. It passed and I deleted it before committing. The .NET 10 Web SDK already generates `public partial class Program` for top-level statements, so I didn't add anything to `Program.cs` or anywhere else. That's the least intrusive option.

## Deviations
- **`global.json` has a `test.runner` setting.** xunit.v3 4.x only runs on Microsoft.Testing.Platform, and without this setting .NET 10's `dotnet test` stops with "Testing with VSTest target is no longer supported". This is the documented opt-in. It means `dotnet test` now uses the new runner's options and output format.
- **With zero tests, `dotnet test` exits 8, not 0.** The new runner treats "zero tests ran" as a failure. This stops once the red suites land. I didn't add `--ignore-exit-code 8` or a minimum-tests setting to the project files, because that would also hide a test project that silently runs nothing later.

## Uncertain
- **CA1707 will block underscore test names.** `AnalysisLevel=latest-recommended` plus `TreatWarningsAsErrors` turns CA1707 into a build error, so names like `Week_spans_dst_change` won't compile; the temporary check hit this. CA1050 (types must be in a namespace) also applies. Either test-author uses PascalCase names, or the coordinator approves a `tests/.editorconfig` with `dotnet_diagnostic.CA1707.severity = none`. I didn't pick either.
- **`xunit.runner.visualstudio` and `Microsoft.NET.Test.Sdk` do nothing for the CLI** now that the new runner is on. I kept them because they were on the required list and they still help some IDE test explorers. They could be dropped.
- **`Api.Tests` references `Testcontainers.MsSql`.** I assumed the Phase 3 golden tests run the API against real SQL Server (CLAUDE.md rule 6). Remove it if the API tests will only use fakes.
- **`.sln` vs `.slnx`:** I used the classic `.sln` format as you asked (`--format sln`). .NET 10 would create `.slnx` by default.
- **Template leftovers:** I kept the template `launchSettings.json` (it has an https profile, port 7071) and the `appsettings*.json` files. The Angular dev proxy will need the http port 5235, or the frontend's own choice, to line up.

## Next
- Architect: put contracts on top of these projects. The references are already in place: Core → none, Infrastructure → Core, Api → Core + Infrastructure. Please don't edit project files; any package or project change goes through the coordinator to `backend`.
- `Program.cs` needs no marker for `WebApplicationFactory&lt;Program&gt;`.
- Test authors: tests must be in a namespace and use PascalCase names unless CA1707 is relaxed (see Uncertain). `TestContext.Current.CancellationToken` works as the xunit.v3 cancellation token.
- The seed files are now at `db/schema.sql` and `db/seed.sql`.
- I didn't touch `web/`, `docs/`, `PLAN.md`, `CLAUDE.md` or the main worktree.</result>

Here's the planned layout, taken from `PLAN.md` §6 and the agent definitions. The top level and the Angular app already exist in the two scaffolds waiting for review. The folders inside the .NET projects get created when the architect adds the contracts.

```
relay-activity-health/
  db/                      schema.sql, seed.sql (starter files, never modified)
  src/
    Relay.Core/            business logic + application services + query interfaces (references nothing)
      Calendar/            IWeekCalendar, WeekWindow, …
      Normality/           IBaselineEvaluator, ILocationRanker, BaselineAssessment, NormalityOptions, HealthStatus
      ActivityHealth/      IActivityHealthService, ActivityHealthReport, LocationHealth, AccountSummary, ActivityHealthResult, …
      Accounts/            IAccountService, AccountListItem
      Queries/             IActivityQueries, IAccountQueries (implemented in Infrastructure)
    Relay.Infrastructure/  EF Core DbContext + config, migrations, hand-written SQL behind the query interfaces
    Relay.Api/             Program.cs, Minimal API endpoints, result→HTTP mapping, DI composition root
  tests/
    Relay.Core.Tests/            unit tests, no DB, no mocks
    Relay.Infrastructure.Tests/  SQL Server via Testcontainers
    Relay.Api.Tests/             API + golden values against the real seed
  web/src/app/
    core/models/           TS contract types (match the API JSON)
    core/api/              abstract ActivityHealthApi / AccountsApi + Http implementations
    features/dashboard/
      dashboard-state.ts   URL ⇄ signals
      dashboard.page.ts    container
      components/          dashboard-filters, account-summary, location-table
      week.ts              pure week helpers
  analysis/, docs/, scripts/, ai-log/   evidence, specs, AI log (not source)
```

- **`Relay.Core`:** folders are by concept, with one public type per file and the namespace matching the folder.
- **Dependencies:** they point one way only, Api → Infrastructure → Core. Core has no dependencies.
- **Not yet pinned down:**
  - the folders inside `Relay.Infrastructure` and `Relay.Api`;
  - whether each test project mirrors its target's folders.

  I plan to have the architect set those when writing the contracts, following the same folder-by-concept rule, and you'll see them in the contract review. If you'd rather choose the structure yourself, for example folders by feature instead of by concept, tell me before the architect starts.

The reviewer is checking both scaffolds now. After that I'll bring you the open decisions in one batch, including the port-5000 conflict and the acceptance-criteria questions.

A few PLAN §7 and brief items have no criterion. Verdict: CHANGES REQUIRED. Every item in section (a) can be fixed without a decision.

## Changed
Nothing edited. I used one throwaway inline Python run over `analysis/statistician/golden.py` and `analysis/reference_model.py` (no files created).

## Verification
I read `docs/acceptance-criteria.md` at aa71832, PLAN §13 (both 2026-09-28 entries), `docs/design-consensus.md`, `../Requirements.md`, `schema.sql` and `seed.sql`. I ran `evaluate`/`show` from `golden.py` on the seed.

**Recomputed and matching:**

- Account 6 week 2026-06-08 (all): 102, range 37–104, `normal`. Site C is 11 vs 1–8 `above` and Site J is 11 vs 2–10 `above`. Site K is `normal`, so 13 sites are `normal`.

## Findings

### (a) Objective defects the product agent can fix without a decision

1. **[Major] §0.1 table header and C-08: copy filed as approved that is not in PLAN.**
   - The table is headed "Approved copy (PLAN §13 §5.4 = consensus §2)". C-08 (`compared with the last 8 full weeks for this account`) appears in neither PLAN §13 §5.4 nor consensus §2.
   - Its only source is a sign-off remark in consensus §9. The remark names the intent ("for this account") but does not fix the string.
   - The frontend is told to copy the string verbatim, so this invents copy under an "approved" label.
   - C-15 ("Viewing as") is also outside §13 §5.4 (it comes from D5, which §13 does not change other than the account default). Its provenance is fine, but the table header is wrong for it.
   - Fix: move C-08 to §0.2 as PROPOSED and quote the consensus §9 note as its source. Change the header to "Approved copy (PLAN §13 §5.4, D5)".

2. **[Major] BL-13 contradicts API-13.**
   - BL-13 says "The spike does not flag the following weeks".
   - API-13 (account 6, week 2026-06-08, the week right after the spike) has Sites C and J flagged `above`. UI-11 and A.5 agree with API-13.
   - BL-13 holds only for the 2026-07-20 scenario, where all 15 sites are `normal`.
   - Fix: scope BL-13 to "in the account 6 week 2026-07-20 scenario, all 15 sites are `normal`". Also say explicitly that flags in the week after the spike (API-13) are expected.

3. **[Major] Tag overreach on values PLAN does not pin.**
   - API-18 and UI-15 tag "count 8" as GOLDEN. PLAN §13 §7 gives only `insufficient_data` and `weeksUsed` 3 for account 8 week 2026-03-02. The 8 comes from `golden_out.md`, which is not PLAN. Under the document's own tag rules that makes it SEED.
   - API-14 and API-10 correctly separate the pinned values from the order-only SEED parts. Apply the same split to API-18 and UI-15.
   - DATA-10 is tagged "SPEC consensus §3; SEED". BL-40 is tagged "GOLDEN / §13 consensus §3". Use one tag each.
   - Why it matters: test authors may assert values that were never promoted into PLAN.

4. **[Major] BL-41 is untestable as written.** "Each case has a test" gives no inputs or expected values.
   - PLAN §7 Calendar also requires "invalid IANA id", which BL-41 omits. This is a coverage gap.
   - Fix: list each case with expected UTC windows and week starts. At minimum:
     - The America/Chicago DST-start and DST-end weeks (2026-03-08 and 2026-11-01) produce windows of 167 h and 169 h. The 2026-03-02 week ends at the DST start and 2026-03-09 begins after it.
     - Phoenix and UTC weeks are 168 h.
     - An event exactly at the boundary instant belongs to the new week.
     - The anchor cases for latest complete week.
     - An invalid IANA id throws or returns an error.
   - Also add "non-Monday" as an explicit `IsWeekStart` case, not only via BL-43.

5. **[Major] BL-11 and BL-14 have no concrete inputs.**
   - BL-11 ("any baseline, count = `high`") is the only upper-edge criterion by name. Give it concrete rows: [11,11,11,8] → 18 is `normal` and → 19 is `above` (high is 18 per the PLAN hand derivation). [2,4,6,20] → 14 / 15 already covers this in BL-04/05.
   - BL-14 ("rounded away from zero") cannot be tested without a midpoint input, and the doc itself notes 0 seed values sit on a midpoint. Either supply a hand-built (evaluator input with a `.xx5` deviation) case, or state that it is verified only at the API-boundary mapper with a constructed value (e.g. a stubbed `1.005` → `1.01`, `-1.005` → `-1.01`).

6. **[Major] DATA criteria and PLAN §5.1 test placement conflict.**
   - DATA-01, DATA-02, DATA-03 and DATA-10 depend on the seed. The "How to verify" line for §2 points at `tests/Relay.Infrastructure.Tests`. PLAN §5.1/§7 says integration tests apply `InitialCreate` only and insert hand-built fixtures.
   - Fix: state which project hosts each. Seed-dependent criteria belong in `Relay.Api.Tests`, or in Infrastructure tests that explicitly run the `LoadSeedData` migration.
   - There is also no hand-built NULL-duplicate criterion. The reviewer checklist and consensus §4.2 require NULL-safe de-dup on every non-id column.
   - Fix: add rows to DATA-02 for identical pairs where `duration_seconds` is NULL, `outcome` is NULL, and both are NULL. Also add pairs that differ only in `location` and only in `event_type` (must not merge). Only those two columns are nullable.
   - DATA-02's "over all rows" phrasing is loose. The weekly query is per account and window, so the total of 12,614 only holds if the windows cover every event. Assert it as `SELECT COUNT(*) FROM (SELECT DISTINCT …)` or per account.

7. **[Minor] DATA-05 covers only the window start.** Add the end: an event exactly at window end is excluded from that window and counted in the next (half-open).

8. **[Minor] DATA-06 asserts an implementation detail.** "`all` is sent to SQL as null" cannot be observed by a behaviour test. Rephrase to the observable result: with `all` every type is counted, with `call_received` only calls are counted, and matching is case-sensitive (collation trap).

9. **[Minor] UI-04 is ambiguous as a test.** The DOM contains no "z" tokens by construction, but a raw substring check for "z" is unreliable. State the check as a word-boundary regex (`\bz\b`, `σ`, `±`, `\bmedian\b`, `\btypical\b`, standalone `\bNormal\b`).

10. **[Minor] UI-38 (replace, not push) is tagged PROPOSED.** It is a reviewer-checklist rule (`replaceUrl` for normalisation) and PLAN §5.4 says the URL is "rewritten". Tag it SPEC.

11. **[Minor] The preamble's "PLAN §13 (2026-09-28 entry)" is ambiguous.** There are two entries dated 2026-09-28. The precedence rules come from the first ("Revised design…"). Name it.

12. **[Minor] UI-35 does not say how the client detects "before earliestWeek".** `earliestWeek` is only known from a successful response, and the API returns 400 for that week. State the expected observable outcome: the UI recovers from the 400 by normalising to the default week (replace), with no error banner. The outcome is already in UI-35, but the mechanism-independent wording is missing.

### Coverage gaps (fixable, no decision needed)

13. **[Major] No criterion for README deliverables.**
    - PLAN §13 requires the README to carry the known limits in `docs/design-consensus.md` §1 verbatim and to state the ≈ 4 % flag rate. The brief requires the README to cover: run instructions, the test-run one-liner, interpretation, assumptions, trade-offs, deferrals, and "what I'd do with another day".
    - Add a README slice with checkable items. Product also owns the README's interpretation section.

14. **[Major] No criterion that the UI prints the API order and API `low`/`high` without recomputation.**
    - C-05 states the rule, but there is no UI criterion that the displayed rows follow the API sort exactly (no client-side re-sorting). The checklist forbids client-side statistics and re-sorting.
    - Add a Vitest/DOM criterion: feed a fixed API payload in a deliberately non-alphabetical, non-deviation order, and assert the rendered row order equals the payload order.

15. **[Minor] API-level gaps.**
    - Unhandled exception: 500 returns `application/problem+json` with no exception message or stack trace (`IExceptionHandler`).
    - A non-numeric `accountId` (`/accounts/abc`) has no criterion; state 404 or 400.
    - A missing `type` param defaults to `all` (only implied by API-10).
    - The `status` strings are exactly `above|below|normal|insufficient_data` and JSON names match PLAN §5.2. No literal-schema criterion pins them.

16. **[Minor] Empty-state derivation.** UI-20 states the outcome. The criterion should also say the empty state is keyed off `locations == [] &amp;&amp; summary.baseline.weeksUsed == 0` (PLAN §13 §5.4), not off `earliestWeek`. It should also say whether "0 inbound events" shows alongside C-07 (currently unspecified).

17. **[Nit] `db/` move and untouched seed/schema files.** There is no criterion that `db/seed.sql` and `db/schema.sql` are moved with content unchanged (a checksum check would suffice).

### (b) Items needing a user or coordinator decision

- **D1. Promote SEED values into PLAN §13?** API-13, API-19 to API-25, UI-11, UI-16 to UI-19 and UI-21 encode useful edge cases with correct values. They cover mixed eligibility, zero-activity sites, and the median ≤ 2 cases where a drop to 0 cannot be `below`. I verified them. Without promotion, test authors are barred from asserting them (per the doc's own tag rules). I recommend adding them, or at least API-19, API-20, API-22, API-23/24 and API-25, as goldens.
- **D2. C-08 wording.** Approve the "for this account" string (or the alternative), or drop it. The consensus §9 note only sets the intent.
- **D3. P-04 column headers.** "This week" is wrong when the user is viewing 2026-06-01, and "Usual range" duplicates the cell text "Usually X–Y a week". Decide the final headers (e.g. "Location", "Events", "Usual range", "Status").
- **D4. UI-39 (account switch).** Decide what happens to `week` and `type` when the account changes. The doc proposes: `week` falls back to the default when it is before the new account's `earliestWeek`, and `type` is kept.
- **D5. Scope of the PROPOSED copy and behaviour.** P-01 to P-09 and UI-40 (loading, error, retry, and type labels) are not in PLAN. They are correctly tagged and none blocks the golden path. Approve the set, or trim to the cut line in PLAN §10.
- **D6. API-46 (malformed `week`, e.g. `20260720`, `2026-13-01`).** The doc treats 400 as the natural reading. Confirm it, since PLAN only specifies the "YYYY-MM-DD" format.
- **D7. UI-01 / UI-32 to UI-37 normalisation target.** All rewrites fall back to the default week (latest complete week). Confirm this is preferred over snapping to the nearest valid Monday.

## Verdict

CHANGES REQUIRED. No wrong GOLDEN or SPEC value was found. The blocking issues are the tag and classification mistakes (items 1 to 3), the untestable criteria (4 and 5), the missing README and UI-ordering criteria (13 and 14), and the PLAN §5.1 test-placement conflict (6).

## Uncertain
- Whether "consensus §2 = PLAN §13 §5.4" for the footnote also permits the "Within usual range" mention the sign-off note says appears in the footnote. The §13 footnote list does not include it, and the doc's UI-06 correctly omits it.
- Whether UI-03 and API-11 should be GOLDEN or SEED for the order of Sites C, A, D (PLAN pins only "ranked first").

## Next
- The product agent fixes section (a), items 1 to 17.
- The coordinator decides D1 to D7. In particular D1 (promotion of SEED values into PLAN §13) unblocks the test-author.

<result>## Summary
I reviewed both Phase 0 scaffolds and ran the builds and tests in scratch copies, so neither worktree was touched. The backend scaffold is structurally sound but has one defect that will break Phase 1, and the proxy and API ports disagree. The frontend shell builds and passes its one test, but it is not in strict mode. The two branches merge cleanly.

## Changed
Nothing. Read-only review. Scratch copies are under the session scratchpad (`.../scratchpad/be` and `.../scratchpad/fe`).

## Verification
- **Backend, build:** `dotnet build` gives 0 warnings and 0 errors.
- **Backend, test:** `dotnet test` prints "Zero tests ran" for each of the three test projects and exits with code 8.
- **Backend analyzer probe:** I added a temporary `Baseline_needs_four_weeks` test in a scratch copy. It fails the build with CA1707. A test class outside a namespace also fails with CA1050. With PascalCase names and a namespace, the build is clean and 2 tests pass.
- **Frontend:** `npm run build` and `npm test` both succeed (1 test passed).
- **Merge:** `git merge-tree` was blocked by the sandbox. Instead I confirmed that the frontend branch touches only `web/`, and the backend branch touches only root files, `src/`, `tests/` and `db/`. No path overlaps.
- **Root `.gitignore`:** it already exists on `main`. The backend branch appends to it and the frontend does not touch it. The frontend's `web/.gitignore` and `web/.editorconfig` are separate files. The root and `web/` `.editorconfig` files both set `root = true`, so `web/` overrides the root file inside its own tree. That is harmless but redundant.

## Deviations
None from the agent files apart from the findings below.

## Findings

### (a) Objective defects the owning agent can fix without a decision

1. **[Blocker for Phase 1] Test projects fail on the first PLAN §7 test name.**
   - `Directory.Build.props:7` sets `AnalysisLevel=latest-recommended` and `TreatWarningsAsErrors=true`, and this applies to the test projects too.
   - Snake-case test names such as `Baseline_needs_four_weeks` fail with CA1707. Types outside a namespace fail with CA1050.
   - Fix (backend): add `&lt;NoWarn&gt;$(NoWarn);CA1707&lt;/NoWarn&gt;` for `tests/**` only, either in `tests/Directory.Build.props` or in the three test csproj files. Keep analyzers on for `src/`. Alternatively, record a PascalCase naming rule for tests in CLAUDE.md, which is a user call (see b1).

2. **[Major] Ports disagree, so the proxy points at the wrong place.**
   - `src/Relay.Api/Properties/launchSettings.json:8` and `:17` use 5235 (http) and 7071 (https). `web/proxy.conf.json` targets `http://localhost:5000`.
   - `frontend.md` says to use :5000 "unless `src/Relay.Api` says otherwise". It does say otherwise, and on this Mac :5000 is AirPlay, so the proxy would silently forward `/api` to AirPlay.
   - Fix (frontend): set the proxy target to `http://localhost:5235`. No decision is needed for the minimum fix. The alternative of picking a different port is in b2.

3. **[Major] Angular strict mode is missing.**
   - `web/tsconfig.json` has no `"strict": true`, and `angularCompilerOptions` has no `"strictTemplates": true`. Both are required by `frontend.md` ("strict mode") and PLAN §13.
   - Without `strictTemplates`, template type errors in the Phase 2 components go unreported.
   - Fix (frontend): add `"strict": true` under `compilerOptions` and `"strictTemplates": true` under `angularCompilerOptions`. Then rebuild and re-run the tests.

4. **[Minor] Template leftovers in `web/`.**
   - `web/public/favicon.ico` is the Angular logo, and `index.html:8` still links it.
   - `web/.prettierrc` and the `prettier` devDependency are template extras that nobody asked for.
   - `web/.editorconfig` duplicates the root file. The root already gives 2-space indent for TS/HTML/JSON.
   - `@angular/forms` is installed and unused. `frontend.md` names no forms package. Remove it, or add it back when Phase 2 needs it.
   - Fix (frontend): remove the unused items.

5. **[Minor] `launchSettings.json` has template extras.**
   - It has `"launchBrowser": true` on both profiles, but the API has no root route, so the browser opens on a 404.
   - The `https` profile and its port 7071 are unused, because `dotnet run` and the proxy use the http profile.
   - Fix (backend): set `launchBrowser` to false and drop the https profile.

6. **[Minor] `web/src/app/app.spec.ts` is generated boilerplate.** It is needed only so Vitest has a file to run, since Vitest fails when it finds no test file. It is not covered by any PLAN edge case, so keep it deliberately as a smoke test or replace it in Phase 2. Nothing to fix now.

### (b) Items needing a user decision

1. **`dotnet test` exits 8 with zero tests.**
   - The runner is `Microsoft.Testing.Platform` (`global.json:7`), and it treats zero tests as an error. `backend.md` says "Done: `dotnet test` runs (0 tests)". It runs, but the coordinator's `&amp;&amp;` chains and CI will see a failure.
   - Options:
     - Accept it, since Phase 1 adds tests immediately.
     - Pass `--ignore-exit-code 8` in the coordinator's command. I confirmed the flag works, but only for the exact syntax `dotnet test --solution Relay.sln --ignore-exit-code 8`.
     - Set `TestingPlatformCommandLineArguments` in `Directory.Build.props`. This is riskier because it would also mask a real "all tests deleted" failure later.
   - My recommendation is to accept it. Note the behaviour in the README or CLAUDE.md so nobody mistakes it for a break.

2. **Which port the API should use.**
   - Options: 5235 (the .NET template default, matching finding a2), or a fixed project port such as 5080 set in both `launchSettings.json` and `proxy.conf.json`.
   - If you want a custom port, choose one, and record the port in one place. Otherwise the two files will drift again.

3. **CA1707 policy for tests.** Suppress it for tests (fix a1, my recommendation, because PLAN §7 edge cases read best as sentences), or require PascalCase names.

4. **Test-project package set.** `Relay.Api.Tests` and `Relay.Infrastructure.Tests` both reference `Testcontainers.MsSql`, and `Api.Tests` also references `Mvc.Testing`. This matches the `backend.md` list, so it is not a defect. Note that `Relay.Api.Tests` cannot use `WebApplicationFactory&lt;Program&gt;` while `Program` is an internal top-level class and `InternalsVisibleTo` is banned. When someone adds the DI calls to `Program.cs`, they will also need `public partial class Program;`. That is a Phase 3 heads-up, not a Phase 0 fix.

## Next
- Backend fixes a1 and a5, and frontend fixes a2, a3 and a4, are all small and independent.
- Merging both branches now is safe, since they touch disjoint paths. The proxy port and the strict-mode flags can be fixed after the merge if you prefer.
- Reviewed by reading `git show` and file contents plus the build and test runs above.
- Not covered: the frontend worktree needs a real `npm start` against a running API to prove the proxy end to end. The proxy target is still 5000 today, so it would fail until a2 is fixed.

## Clean areas
- **Project graph and boundaries:** clean. Core has no references, Infrastructure references Core, Api references Core and Infrastructure, and each test project references only its own target. There is no `InternalsVisibleTo`.
- **Central package management:** clean. `ManagePackageVersionsCentrally` is on, versions live only in `Directory.Packages.props`, and there are no banned packages (no FluentAssertions, mapper, mediator or Moq). EF Design is `PrivateAssets=all`.
- **`global.json` and build settings:** clean. SDK 10.0.201 with `rollForward: latestFeature`, `net10.0`, nullable and implicit usings on, warnings treated as errors, and a 0-warning build.
- **`db/` files:** clean. Both are pure renames (0 line changes, similarity 100%).
- **Comment blocks and stray files:** clean. I found none in either branch.
- **No contracts or logic added:** clean. `Program.cs` is a 5-line host, the Core csproj is empty, and the frontend `DashboardPage` is an empty template.
- **Angular structure:** clean. Everything is standalone with `OnPush`. There is no SSR or `NgModule`, and routing has one route with a redirect. Vitest runs through `@angular/build:unit-test`, and `serve` uses the proxy config.
- **Scope:** clean. The frontend touches only `web/`, and the backend touches only the root project files, `src/`, `tests/` and `db/`.
- **Merge:** clean, with no overlapping paths.</result>

<result>## Summary
Every public contract for DASH-247 is in place with `NotImplementedException` stubs, following PLAN §6 as superseded by §13: Core, Infrastructure, Api and Angular. Both stacks build with 0 warnings and both test runs pass. I didn't touch any project or package file.

- **Worktree:** `/Users/josemejia/Documents/Software Development/Qualitara/relay-activity-health/.claude/worktrees/agent-a9154856706d28a3b`
- **Branch:** `worktree-agent-a9154856706d28a3b`, commit `d96a3f1`

## Changed
- `src/Relay.Core/{Calendar,Normality,ActivityHealth,Accounts,Queries}/*.cs`: 30 files, one public type per file
- `src/Relay.Infrastructure/{Composition,Persistence,Queries}/*.cs`: 4 files
- `src/Relay.Api/{Composition,Endpoints,Http}/*.cs`: 17 files, plus an edited `src/Relay.Api/Program.cs`
- `web/src/app/core/models/*.ts` (10), `web/src/app/core/api/{activity-health,accounts}.api.ts`, `web/src/app/features/dashboard/dashboard-state.ts`, and an edited `web/src/app/app.config.ts`

**Folder layout (one rule everywhere: folder = concept, namespace = folder, one public type per file):**
```
src/Relay.Core/            Calendar/  Normality/  ActivityHealth/  Accounts/  Queries/
src/Relay.Infrastructure/  Composition/ (DI)  Persistence/ (DbContext, later entities + configs)  Queries/ (Sql* implementations)  Migrations/ (database agent, EF-generated)
src/Relay.Api/             Composition/ (DI incl. AddRelayCore)  Endpoints/ (route mapping + handlers)  Http/ (request/response DTOs, bindable types, mapping extensions)
web/src/app/               core/models/  core/api/  features/dashboard/
```

## Verification
- `dotnet build --no-incremental` gives `0 Warning(s) 0 Error(s)`.
- `dotnet test` gives `total: 0 … non-success exit code: 8`, which is the accepted outcome with no tests yet.
- `cd web &amp;&amp; npm ci &amp;&amp; npm run build` gives "Application bundle generation complete", with no warnings.
- `npx tsc -p tsconfig.app.json --noEmit` exits 0. This covers `dashboard-state.ts`, which nothing imports yet.
- `npm test` gives "Test Files 1 passed, Tests 1 passed" (the scaffold's `app.spec.ts`).
- **Throwaway `WebApplicationFactory&lt;Program&gt;` tests, run then deleted before the commit:** 5/5 passed.
  - `type=ALL` and `type=Call_Received` return 400 `application/problem+json`, so `AddValidation()` and the regex attribute work.
  - `/api/accounts/abc/activity-health` returns 404 `application/problem+json`.
  - A config override `Normality:BaselineWeeks=3` or `Normality:SpreadFloor=0` makes startup throw `OptionsValidationException`. That proves binding to the init setters works and `ValidateOnStart` is active.
  - `Program` is reachable from `Relay.Api.Tests` without `InternalsVisibleTo` or a `public partial class Program`, because the .NET 10 generator makes it public.

## Deviations
1. **`AddRelayCore` lives in `Relay.Api/Composition`, not in `Relay.Core`.** Core has no package references, and registering it needs the `Microsoft.Extensions.*` packages. I kept Core pure instead of asking for packages. Core classes take `NormalityOptions` as plain data (`BaselineEvaluator`, `ActivityHealthService`). The composition root exposes it with `AddSingleton(sp =&gt; IOptions&lt;NormalityOptions&gt;.Value)`. This differs from `backend.md`, which says constants come "only from `IOptions&lt;NormalityOptions&gt;`". If you want the conventional layout instead, `backend` would add `Microsoft.Extensions.Options.ConfigurationExtensions` and `Microsoft.Extensions.Options.DataAnnotations` to Core, and I'd move the method.
2. **`RelayDbContext` is only a constructor shell.** Entities, DbSets, configurations and migrations belong to `database`. `AddRelayInfrastructure` uses the connection string name `"Relay"` (matching `database.md`), and it isn't in the appsettings yet.
3. **`IsoDate` (Api/Http) is a bindable type with a stubbed `TryParse`**, so the `week` parameter only accepts `yyyy-MM-dd`. Plain `DateOnly` binding would also accept formats like `07/20/2026`.

## Uncertain
- **`AccountSummary` means the account-level summary row** (`Count` plus `BaselineAssessment`), which matches the JSON `summary`. The account identity is `AccountListItem(Id, Name, TimeZoneId)`, used both by the list endpoint and by `ActivityHealthReport.Account`. Please confirm that reading.
- **The grid carries more than PLAN §6 lists.** I added the records `WeeklySeries` and `LocationSeries`. Eligibility can be computed from `WeekWindow.StartUtc` alone (eligible ⇔ first activity &lt; window start), so the grid doesn't depend on the calendar.
- **`IWeekCalendar` takes `string timeZoneId`.** How it handles an invalid IANA id (throw `TimeZoneNotFoundException` or something else) is for test-author and backend to pin down.
- **`FindDataAnchorAsync` returns `DateTime?`.** What the service does when there are no events at all isn't specified by PLAN.
- **Records containing `IReadOnlyList` compare lists by reference.** Tests should compare list contents, not whole records.
- **`HealthStatus` and `ActivityType` are enums, and the DTOs carry strings** (`"insufficient_data"`, `"call_received"`). The mapping in `ToResponse`/`ToQuery` is backend's job, as is rounding `deviation` to 2 decimal places away from zero.
- **`DashboardState` is `@Injectable()` but not provided anywhere yet.** Frontend decides where it's provided, most likely in `DashboardPage`'s providers.

## Next
- **backend:** fill in the Core stubs; the Api handlers, `ToQuery`, `ToResponse`, `ToHttpResult` and `IsoDate.TryParse`; and the migrate-at-startup step.
- **database:** add entities and configurations to `RelayDbContext`, the `Relay` connection string, and `SqlActivityQueries`/`SqlAccountQueries`. The sites query takes no `beforeUtc`. `CountWeeklyBySiteAsync` returns only non-zero rows keyed by `WeekWindow.WeekStart`, and `ActivityType.All` maps to SQL `null`.
- **test-author:** can write the red suites against the listing below now.

## Decision validation
1. **Port 5080: VALID**, no contract impact.
2. **PascalCase test names, CA1707 on: VALID.** It passes under `latest-recommended`, and no contract name has an underscore.
3. **`dotnet test` exit 8 accepted: VALID**, and it's what I observed.
4. **Ten promoted goldens: VALID.** The contracts can express them all:
   - site inclusion uses `first &lt; selectedWeek.EndUtc` (acct 14, 2026-01-26: only B and D listed);
   - there's a `WeeksUsed` field for every insufficient-data row;
   - the ranker covers the name tie-break (the `appointment_set` ties).
5. **Malformed week → 400, non-numeric id → 404: VALID, contract impact applied.** `IsoDate` binding covers the week, and `{accountId:int}` is in place; the 404 was verified by the throwaway test.
6. **Invalid URL param reset to its default with `replaceUrl`: VALID** (PLAN §5.4). `DashboardState.week` is `string | null`; `null` means "ask the API without `week`" and then rewrite the URL from `latestCompleteWeek`.
7. **Account switch keeps week and type, falls back on "before earliestWeek": VALID.** One mechanism note: the UI only learns the new account's `earliestWeek` from the response. So the fallback has to be triggered by the 400 for the kept week. Type is validated on the client, so any 400 there is caused by the week. There's no API impact; adding `earliestWeek` to `GET /api/accounts` would conflict with §13 §5.2.
8. **Empty account message: VALID.** No impact; the existing fields cover it (`locations == [] &amp;&amp; summary.baseline.weeksUsed == 0`, `earliestWeek == latestCompleteWeek`).
9. **Extra copy: VALID, contract impact applied.** I added `DashboardState.reload()` for the "Try again" button. The labels are frontend/product copy, so nothing in the models changes.
10. **Footnote capitalisation: VALID**, no impact.
11. **Type-specific nouns ("12 calls"): VALID.** No impact; the report carries `eventType`.
12. **"0 of 4 weeks needed" kept: VALID.** It's consistent with §13 §5.4 (N = `baseline.weeksUsed`).

## Public contract listing

**Relay.Core**
```csharp
// Calendar
record WeekWindow(DateOnly WeekStart, DateTime StartUtc, DateTime EndUtc);
interface IWeekCalendar {
  bool IsWeekStart(DateOnly localDate);
  DateOnly WeekContaining(DateTime instantUtc, string timeZoneId);
  DateOnly LatestCompleteWeek(DateTime dataAnchorUtc, string timeZoneId);
  WeekWindow Window(DateOnly weekStart, string timeZoneId);
  IReadOnlyList&lt;WeekWindow&gt; BaselineWindows(DateOnly selectedWeek, int baselineWeeks, string timeZoneId); }
sealed class WeekCalendar : IWeekCalendar  // no dependencies

// Normality
enum HealthStatus { Normal, Above, Below, InsufficientData }
sealed record NormalityOptions { const SectionName = "Normality"; int BaselineWeeks = 8; int MinimumEligibleWeeks = 4; double BandWidth = 2.0; double SpreadFloor = 1.0; } // init props, [Range]
record BaselineAssessment(int WeeksUsed, double? Median, int? Low, int? High, HealthStatus Status, double? Deviation);
record SeriesWeek(DateOnly WeekStart, int Count, bool IsEligible);
record WeeklySeries(int SelectedWeekCount, IReadOnlyList&lt;SeriesWeek&gt; BaselineWeeks);
record LocationSeries(string Location, WeeklySeries Series);
interface IWeeklyGridBuilder {
  IReadOnlyList&lt;LocationSeries&gt; BuildLocationSeries(WeekWindow selectedWeek, IReadOnlyList&lt;WeekWindow&gt; baselineWindows, IReadOnlyList&lt;SiteFirstActivity&gt; siteFirstActivities, IReadOnlyList&lt;WeeklySiteCount&gt; weeklySiteCounts);
  WeeklySeries BuildAccountSeries(/* same parameters */); }
interface IBaselineEvaluator { BaselineAssessment Evaluate(IReadOnlyList&lt;int&gt; eligibleWeekCounts, int selectedWeekCount); }
interface ILocationRanker { IReadOnlyList&lt;LocationHealth&gt; Rank(IReadOnlyList&lt;LocationHealth&gt; locations); }
sealed class WeeklyGridBuilder; sealed class BaselineEvaluator(NormalityOptions); sealed class LocationRanker;

// ActivityHealth
enum ActivityType { All, CallReceived, LeadCreated, AppointmentSet }
record ActivityHealthQuery(int AccountId, DateOnly? Week, ActivityType EventType);
record LocationHealth(string Location, int Count, BaselineAssessment Baseline);
record AccountSummary(int Count, BaselineAssessment Baseline);
record ActivityHealthReport(AccountListItem Account, ActivityType EventType, DateOnly WeekStart, DateOnly WeekEnd, DateTime DataAsOfUtc,
  DateOnly LatestCompleteWeek, DateOnly EarliestWeek, int BaselineWeeks, int MinimumEligibleWeeks, AccountSummary Summary, IReadOnlyList&lt;LocationHealth&gt; Locations);
abstract record ActivityHealthResult { private ctor;
  sealed record Found(ActivityHealthReport Report); sealed record AccountNotFound; sealed record InvalidWeek(string Reason); }
interface IActivityHealthService { Task&lt;ActivityHealthResult&gt; GetAsync(ActivityHealthQuery query, CancellationToken cancellationToken); }
sealed class ActivityHealthService(IAccountQueries, IActivityQueries, IWeekCalendar, IWeeklyGridBuilder, IBaselineEvaluator, ILocationRanker, NormalityOptions)

// Accounts
record AccountListItem(int Id, string Name, string TimeZoneId);
interface IAccountService { Task&lt;IReadOnlyList&lt;AccountListItem&gt;&gt; ListAsync(CancellationToken cancellationToken); }
sealed class AccountService(IAccountQueries)

// Queries
record SiteFirstActivity(string Location, DateTime FirstActivityUtc);
record WeeklySiteCount(string Location, DateOnly WeekStart, int Count);
interface IActivityQueries {
  Task&lt;DateTime?&gt; FindDataAnchorAsync(CancellationToken cancellationToken);
  Task&lt;IReadOnlyList&lt;SiteFirstActivity&gt;&gt; ListSiteFirstActivitiesAsync(int accountId, CancellationToken cancellationToken);
  Task&lt;IReadOnlyList&lt;WeeklySiteCount&gt;&gt; CountWeeklyBySiteAsync(int accountId, IReadOnlyList&lt;WeekWindow&gt; weekWindows, ActivityType eventType, CancellationToken cancellationToken); }
interface IAccountQueries {
  Task&lt;IReadOnlyList&lt;AccountListItem&gt;&gt; ListAsync(CancellationToken cancellationToken);
  Task&lt;AccountListItem?&gt; FindAsync(int accountId, CancellationToken cancellationToken); }
```
All records are `sealed`, and every implementation class is `sealed`, takes its dependencies through the constructor, and throws `NotImplementedException`.

**Relay.Infrastructure**
```csharp
sealed class RelayDbContext(DbContextOptions&lt;RelayDbContext&gt;) : DbContext;
sealed class SqlActivityQueries(RelayDbContext) : IActivityQueries;
sealed class SqlAccountQueries(RelayDbContext) : IAccountQueries;
static IServiceCollection AddRelayInfrastructure(this IServiceCollection, IConfiguration); // AddDbContext + UseSqlServer("Relay"), scoped queries
```

**Relay.Api**
```csharp
static IServiceCollection AddRelayCore(this IServiceCollection, IConfiguration);  // options bind + DataAnnotations + Min&lt;=Baseline + ValidateOnStart; calendar/grid/evaluator/ranker singleton; services scoped
static IServiceCollection AddRelayApi(this IServiceCollection);                   // AddProblemDetails, AddValidation
static IEndpointRouteBuilder MapRelayEndpoints(this IEndpointRouteBuilder);       // → MapAccountEndpoints, MapActivityHealthEndpoints
GET /api/accounts                                  → Task&lt;Ok&lt;IReadOnlyList&lt;AccountResponse&gt;&gt;&gt; ListAccounts(IAccountService, CancellationToken)
GET /api/accounts/{accountId:int}/activity-health  → Task&lt;Results&lt;Ok&lt;ActivityHealthResponse&gt;, ProblemHttpResult&gt;&gt; GetActivityHealth([AsParameters] ActivityHealthRequest, IActivityHealthService, CancellationToken)
record ActivityHealthRequest([FromRoute] int AccountId, [FromQuery] IsoDate? Week, [FromQuery, RegularExpression("^(all|call_received|lead_created|appointment_set)$")] string? Type);
readonly record struct IsoDate(DateOnly Value) { static bool TryParse(string? value, IFormatProvider? provider, out IsoDate isoDate); }
record ActivityHealthResponse(AccountResponse Account, string EventType, WeekRangeResponse Week, DateTime DataAsOf, DateOnly LatestCompleteWeek,
  DateOnly EarliestWeek, int BaselineWeeks, int MinimumEligibleWeeks, SummaryResponse Summary, IReadOnlyList&lt;LocationHealthResponse&gt; Locations);
record AccountResponse(int Id, string Name, string Timezone);
record WeekRangeResponse(DateOnly Start, DateOnly End);
record BaselineResponse(int WeeksUsed, double? Median, int? Low, int? High);
record SummaryResponse(int Count, BaselineResponse Baseline, string Status, double? Deviation);
record LocationHealthResponse(string Location, int Count, BaselineResponse Baseline, string Status, double? Deviation);
static ActivityHealthQuery ToQuery(this ActivityHealthRequest);
static Results&lt;Ok&lt;ActivityHealthResponse&gt;, ProblemHttpResult&gt; ToHttpResult(this ActivityHealthResult);
static ActivityHealthResponse ToResponse(this ActivityHealthReport);
static AccountResponse ToResponse(this AccountListItem);
```
`Program.cs` contains only `AddRelayCore`, `AddRelayInfrastructure`, `AddRelayApi`, `UseExceptionHandler()`, `UseStatusCodePages()`, `MapRelayEndpoints()` and `Run()`.

**TypeScript (`web/src/app`)**
```ts
interface Account { id: number; name: string; timezone: string }
type EventType = 'all' | 'call_received' | 'lead_created' | 'appointment_set';  const EVENT_TYPES: readonly EventType[]
type HealthStatus = 'above' | 'below' | 'normal' | 'insufficient_data'
interface Baseline { weeksUsed: number; median: number | null; low: number | null; high: number | null }
interface WeekRange { start: string; end: string }
interface AccountHealthSummary { count: number; baseline: Baseline; status: HealthStatus; deviation: number | null }
interface LocationHealth { location: string; count: number; baseline: Baseline; status: HealthStatus; deviation: number | null }
interface ActivityHealthReport { account: Account; eventType: EventType; week: WeekRange; dataAsOf: string; latestCompleteWeek: string;
  earliestWeek: string; baselineWeeks: number; minimumEligibleWeeks: number; summary: AccountHealthSummary; locations: LocationHealth[] }
interface ProblemDetails { type?; title?; status?; detail?; instance?; errors?: Record&lt;string, string[]&gt; }
interface ActivityHealthRequest { accountId: number; week: string | null; eventType: EventType }
abstract class ActivityHealthApi { abstract getActivityHealth(request: ActivityHealthRequest): Observable&lt;ActivityHealthReport&gt; }  // HttpActivityHealthApi stub
abstract class AccountsApi { abstract listAccounts(): Observable&lt;Account[]&gt; }                                                   // HttpAccountsApi stub
// app.config.ts: provideHttpClient(), ActivityHealthApi → HttpActivityHealthApi, AccountsApi → HttpAccountsApi
const DEFAULT_ACCOUNT_ID = 14; const DEFAULT_EVENT_TYPE: EventType = 'all';
@Injectable() class DashboardState {
  accountId: Signal&lt;number&gt;; week: Signal&lt;string | null&gt;; eventType: Signal&lt;EventType&gt;;
  accounts: Signal&lt;readonly Account[]&gt;; report: Signal&lt;ActivityHealthReport | undefined&gt;; isLoading: Signal&lt;boolean&gt;; error: Signal&lt;ProblemDetails | null&gt;;
  selectAccount(accountId: number): void; selectWeek(week: string): void; selectEventType(eventType: EventType): void; reload(): void }
```
The `DashboardState` signals are lazy `computed` values that throw when read, and the methods throw `Error('Not implemented')`.</result>

<result>## Verdict: CHANGES REQUIRED

There are no blockers. `dotnet build` gives 0 warnings and 0 errors. `npm run build` succeeds, and `npm test` passes 1 test (from the frontend scaffold, not the architect). I read every changed file in `d96a3f1` (`git diff main...worktree-agent-a9154856706d28a3b`). I found no comment blocks, no logic, SQL or tests, and no `.sln`, `.csproj` or `package.json` changes. Core references nothing. Every implementation class is sealed and constructor-injected, and every body throws `NotImplementedException` (or `Error('Not implemented')` in TS).

## Handoff

**Summary.** The Phase 0 contracts are structurally sound and can express all §13 goldens and edge cases. One real HTTP-validation defect (empty `type=`) and several contract ambiguities need fixing before the red suites are written against them.

**Changed.** Nothing (read-only). I used a scratch copy at `/private/tmp/claude-501/-Users-josemejia-Documents-Software-Development-Qualitara-relay-activity-health/485c1256-5727-457d-8aec-65bc845ee171/scratchpad/r`.

**Verification.**
- `dotnet build`: 0 warnings, 0 errors.
- `npm ci &amp;&amp; npm run build &amp;&amp; npm test`: build succeeded, 1 test passed.
- I ran the built API on `127.0.0.1:5199` and probed `type` validation (results under A1).

**Deviations.** These are the architect's three, judged below.

**Uncertain.** My verdicts on the architect's Uncertain items, below. One item I could not verify (B4).

**Next.** Fix A1–A6 and get decisions on B1–B6 before test-author starts.

## Findings

### (a) Objective defects the architect can fix without a decision

**A1. [Major] Empty `type=` is accepted.**
- Evidence: `src/Relay.Api/Http/ActivityHealthRequest.cs:8`. The API ran with the contract as-is (the handler stub throws), and:

| Request | Result |
|---|---|
| `type=` | passes validation, reaches the handler |
| `type=ALL`, `type=bogus`, `type=%20all`, `type=all%0A` | 400 with ProblemDetails |

- Why: `RegularExpressionAttribute` treats null and empty as valid. PLAN §13 §5.2 says "Exactly `all` | ... else 400", so `?type=` must be a 400.
- Fix: add `[MinLength(1)]`, or bind `Type` as a strict parsed type like `IsoDate`.
- Also: the regex string duplicates the four wire names that the enum mapping must know (see B2). Derive both from one source.

**A2. [Major] `InvalidWeek(string Reason)` gives test-author nothing to assert on.**
- Evidence: `src/Relay.Core/ActivityHealth/ActivityHealthResult.cs:13`.
- Why: PLAN §13 has three distinct 400 causes: not a Monday, after `latestCompleteWeek`, before `earliestWeek`. A free-text reason forces string-matching tests, which are coupled to the implementation (reviewer checklist §5).
- Fix: `enum InvalidWeekReason { NotAWeekStart, AfterLatestCompleteWeek, BeforeEarliestWeek }`, with `InvalidWeek(InvalidWeekReason Reason)`. The mapper builds the ProblemDetails `detail` from it. Plain data, no logic. The architect owns the change.

**A3. [Major] `IWeekCalendar` failure behaviour is unspecified.**
- Evidence: `src/Relay.Core/Calendar/IWeekCalendar.cs`.
- Gaps: PLAN §7 requires a calendar test for "invalid IANA id", and the contract does not say what is thrown. Nothing says what `Window()` or `BaselineWindows()` do for a non-Monday input.
- Fix: name the exception in the contract. Options are a Core-defined `UnknownTimeZoneException(string TimeZoneId)`, or the BCL `TimeZoneNotFoundException` stated in the handoff. Add a Monday-precondition rule. Comments are forbidden, so pin the behaviour with a named exception type.

**A4. [Minor] Circular namespace dependency inside Core.**
- Evidence: `Normality/ILocationRanker.cs` uses `ActivityHealth.LocationHealth`, while `ActivityHealth/*` uses `Normality.BaselineAssessment`.
- Also: `Queries` depends on `ActivityHealth.ActivityType`, and `WeeklySiteCount` sits in `Queries` while the grid builder consumes it.
- Fix: move `LocationHealth` and `AccountSummary` into `Normality`, or move `ActivityType` to a neutral namespace. This is a small move now and expensive after tests exist.

**A5. [Minor] `WeeklySeries.BaselineWeeks` clashes with `ActivityHealthReport.BaselineWeeks`.**
- Evidence: `src/Relay.Core/Normality/WeeklySeries.cs:3` is `IReadOnlyList&lt;SeriesWeek&gt;`; `src/Relay.Core/ActivityHealth/ActivityHealthReport.cs:13` is `int`. The same domain word means two things.
- Fix: rename the list to `Weeks`, or the int to `BaselineWeekCount`.

**A6. [Minor] Two cosmetic mismatches.**
- `HealthStatus.Normal = 0` makes `default(HealthStatus)` silently mean `Normal`. Reorder so the zero value is not a "healthy" answer, or put `InsufficientData` first.
- The Core `AccountSummary` maps to the JSON `summary` and the API type `SummaryResponse`. It also shares its name with the PLAN §5.4 Angular component `AccountSummary`. Rename it to something like `AccountTotal`, or `SummaryResponse` to match.

### (b) Items needing a user decision

**B1. Deviation 1 (`AddRelayCore` in `Relay.Api/Composition`, plain `NormalityOptions` in Core). Recommend accept.**
- It preserves "Core references nothing", and `NormalityOptions` is plain data that is validated at startup.
- Consequences: `ValidateOnStart` is preserved, and the extra `MinimumEligibleWeeks &lt;= BaselineWeeks` check is a sensible addition.
- Needed: `architect.md` and `backend.md` must be amended, because they say `AddRelayCore` lives in Core.
- Risk: a future test project that wires Core without the Api project must replicate the registrations.

**B2. Enum-to-string mapping has no single owner.**
- The wire names `call_received` and `insufficient_data` are needed in three places: the Api mapper both ways, the Api validation regex, and Infrastructure SQL (`event_type` values).
- Recommendation: one Core-owned name mapping for `ActivityType` and `HealthStatus`. The DB values equal the API values, so Core can own them without leaking infrastructure.
- Alternative: keep them in Api and Infrastructure separately, accepting duplication and drift.
- Not a defect today, but it must be settled before backend and database start.

**B3. Deviation 3 (custom `IsoDate` bindable type). Accept, with a caveat.**
- Binding did reach `IsoDate.TryParse` in my probe. `week=abc` produced a 500 only because the stub throws.
- Alternative: `string? Week` plus validation. That keeps the request DTO free of a custom type but moves parsing into the mapper.
- Caveat: strictness (`yyyy-MM-dd` only, no `2026-7-20`, no whitespace) must be in the test-author's red suite.

**B4. Malformed `week` may not return 400 in Development. Not verified.**
- Minimal APIs default `ThrowOnBadRequest` to true in Development, and `WebApplicationFactory` defaults to Development.
- A failed `TryParse` binding then throws `BadHttpRequestException`, which passes through `UseExceptionHandler`. Whether that ends as 400 ProblemDetails or 500 was not checked. Write tools were denied, so I could not patch the stub in the scratch copy to test it.
- Recommendation: make the architect or backend prove it. Otherwise handle it explicitly (a strict-parse validation attribute, or a `BadHttpRequestException` mapping in the `IExceptionHandler`). PLAN §7 lists "malformed week" explicitly.

**B5. Value-equality caveat on records holding `IReadOnlyList` (`ActivityHealthReport`, `WeeklySeries`, `LocationSeries`, `ActivityHealthResponse`).**
- `Assert.Equal(expectedRecord, actual)` compares list references, so it fails even when the contents match.
- Recommendation: tell test-author to assert per element, or per scalar member. Do not swap in `ImmutableArray`, which is also reference-equal.
- The other choice is to accept this and state it in the handoff.

**B6. Judgments on the remaining Uncertain items.**

| Item | Verdict |
|---|---|
| `IWeekCalendar` taking `string timeZoneId` | Accept. It is pure data and DST correctness stays inside the calendar. |
| `AccountSummary` meaning (count + baseline assessment) | Accept the concept; rename per A6. Note `IAccountService` is not in PLAN §6; it is needed for CLAUDE's "one service per endpoint" rule, and PLAN should record it. |
| Extra grid records `WeeklySeries`, `LocationSeries`, `SeriesWeek` | Accept. They are necessary, and eligibility is derivable from `WeekWindow.StartUtc &gt; firstActivityUtc`, so the builder needs no calendar. Rename per A5. |
| `FindDataAnchorAsync` returning `DateTime?` | Accept, but decide the null case. An empty database (null anchor) has no `ActivityHealthResult` variant. Options: an explicit variant, or document that `AccountNotFound` is not to be reused. |
| `DateTime` (Kind = Utc) for `dataAsOf` and `WeekWindow` | Fragile. `DateTime` serialises with `Z` only if `Kind == Utc`, and `SqlQuery` returns `Unspecified`. `DateTimeOffset` in the API response would remove the risk. PLAN §5.1 says "instants read back are marked Utc", so this is a decision. |

## Hand-verified expressiveness (golden and edge cases against the contracts)

- **Eligibility.** A week is eligible iff `WeekWindow.StartUtc &gt; firstActivityUtc`. A first event exactly at Monday 00:00 is in week W, so W is ineligible, which matches the rule. Contracts can express it: OK.
- **Account 14, 2026-01-26 (only B and D listed).** The site list comes from `ListSiteFirstActivitiesAsync` with no type parameter, filtered by `selectedWeek.EndUtc`. So the type filter never changes the site list: OK.
- **Account 8, week 2026-03-02.** The evaluator takes eligible counts, so `weeksUsed` = count of eligible weeks (3), and `BaselineAssessment(WeeksUsed, null, null, null, InsufficientData, null)` expresses insufficient data: OK.
- **Account 20.** No site rows gives empty locations and `weeksUsed 0`; `earliestWeek = latestCompleteWeek` is service logic; 400 for `week=2026-03-02` is derivable via `EarliestWeek`: OK (needs A2's reason enum for a clean test).
- **Ties (account 14, `appointment_set`).** `LocationHealth` carries unrounded `Deviation` and `Status`, so the ranker can order below before above, then by name: OK. `BaselineAssessment.Deviation` is unrounded and rounding is left to the API mapper: OK.
- **Response shape.** `BaselineResponse(int WeeksUsed, double? Median, int? Low, int? High)` is always present. Null members serialise as `null` under the default web JSON options. `minimumEligibleWeeks`, `latestCompleteWeek` and `earliestWeek` are present, `week.start/end` are `DateOnly`, and status/eventType are strings: OK. Median `66` serialises as `66`, which is valid JSON for the number.

- **Type validation, case-sensitive.** `ALL` and `bogus` return 400. `type=` is not rejected (A1).

<result>## Summary
Re-reviewed branch `worktree-agent-a9154856706d28a3b` (24d51b2) against main. A1–A6 are fixed and the §13 decisions are reflected. I built it and ran the API in a scratch copy. There are no blockers, but two objective items should be fixed or written down before the red test suite is built on these contracts.

## Changed
Nothing. Read-only. The scratch copy is in the scratchpad `wt/` folder.

## Verification
- `git archive` of the branch into scratch, then `dotnet build Relay.sln`: Build succeeded, 0 warnings, 0 errors.
- I ran the API with a dummy connection string and curled `GET /api/accounts/14/activity-health`. The launch settings override the port to 5080.

| Query | Result |
|---|---|
| `week=abc` | 400 `application/problem+json`, `errors.Week: "must be a date in yyyy-MM-dd format"` |
| `type=` | 400, `errors.Type: minimum length of '1'` |
| `type=bogus` | 400, regex message |
| `type=ALL` | 400, regex message |
| `week=2026-7-20` | 400 |
| `week=2026-02-30` | 400 |
| `week=` | 400 |
| `week=2026-07-20&amp;type=all` | 500 problem+json, no exception details (the handler is still a stub) |

- `git diff --name-only` filtered for csproj/sln/props/package/angular/appsettings: nothing touched.
- `git diff` for any comment lines (`//`, `/*`, `///`): none.

## Fix verification (1)
- **A1:** OK. `[MinLength(1), RegularExpression(ExactMatchPattern)]` gives 400 for `type=`, proven by the run. `RegularExpression` alone would have passed an empty string.
- **A2:** OK. `InvalidWeek(InvalidWeekReason)` with `NotAWeekStart`, `AfterLatestCompleteWeek` and `BeforeEarliestWeek`.
- **A3:** OK as a contract, with one gap.
  - `NotAWeekStartException : ArgumentException` exists with a `LocalDate` property.
  - `TimeZoneNotFoundException` is the BCL type.
  - The failure contract is not written in any test or in PLAN, so it is discoverable only by reading the exception type. Comments are forbidden, so the red suite must pin it.
- **A4:** OK. The `using` graph is acyclic:
  - Calendar depends on nothing.
  - Queries depends on Calendar (`WeekWindow`).
  - Normality depends on Calendar and Queries.
  - Accounts depends on Queries.
  - ActivityHealth depends on Accounts-free Queries, Normality and Calendar.
  - Nothing points back.
- **A5:** OK. `WeeklySeries(SelectedWeekCount, PrecedingWeeks)`.
- **A6:** OK. `HealthStatus.InsufficientData` is the zero value. `AccountTotal` replaces the old name.

## Decisions reflected (2)
- `AddRelayCore` lives in `Relay.Api/Composition`, with plain `NormalityOptions` registered as a singleton from `IOptions`. Core takes no package references, and validation runs on start. OK.
- `ActivityTypeNames` and `HealthStatusNames` are in Core. The `type` regex is built from the constants, and `ToResponse` is stubbed against them. OK.
- Strict `yyyy-MM-dd` week validation is proven above.
- Instants are `DateTime`.
- `dataAsOf` is nullable in C# (`DateTime? DataAsOfUtc`, `DateTime? DataAsOf`) and TypeScript (`string | null`).
- `TimeProvider` is injected into `ActivityHealthService`, and `TimeProvider.System` is registered.

## Findings

### (a) Objective
1. **[Major] `dataAsOf` "Z" is not guaranteed by the contract.**
   - Evidence: `WeekWindow`, `SiteFirstActivity` and `ActivityHealthResponse.DataAsOf` are plain `DateTime`, and `FindDataAnchorAsync` returns `DateTime?`.
   - Failing input: a value read via `SqlQuery&lt;DateTime&gt;` has `Kind=Unspecified`, and System.Text.Json then emits `2026-07-27T22:20:34` with no `Z`. That breaks §5.2.
   - Fix: the red suite must include an API golden test asserting the exact `"…Z"` string. That test is already promised in §13. The implementer must apply `DateTime.SpecifyKind(…, Utc)` once, at the Infrastructure boundary or in `ToResponse`.
   - Not a contract-shape defect, but it is a known trap.
2. **[Minor] PLAN §5.2 (around line 301) is not updated for the nullable `dataAsOf`.** The §13 entry says it is "nullable in §5.2", but the example and field table are unchanged. Update the PLAN (governance), or the spec and code will disagree.
3. **[Minor] The calendar failure contract is not pinned anywhere.**
   - `IWeekCalendar` promises `TimeZoneNotFoundException` and `NotAWeekStartException`, but only the exception class exists.
   - The test suite must assert both. Also assert that the service maps `NotAWeekStart` before calling the calendar with a non-Monday.
4. **[Nit] Validation error keys are PascalCase (`Week`, `Type`) while the wire names are camelCase.** This is a `ProblemDetails.errors` key concern only, and the frontend's `ProblemDetails.errors` is a free-form record, so it is harmless. Pin the actual behaviour in the API tests, not an assumed `week`.

### (b) Needs a user decision
1. **Judgement on `IsoDateAttribute.IsValid`: acceptable, keep it in the contract layer.**
   - It is a single `TryParseExact` on a syntactic format. It has no calendar knowledge; Monday, future and earliest-week checks remain in Core (`InvalidWeekReason`). It is what gives the proven 400 through `AddValidation`.
   - Moving it to backend would just re-create the same attribute.
   - Ask for two things: (i) the backend `ToQuery` must reuse `IsoDateAttribute.Format` rather than a second literal, and (ii) a unit test on the attribute in the red suite (null, `abc`, `2026-7-20`, `2026-02-30`, `20260720`).
2. **`?week=` (present but empty) returns 400.** `?type=` is deliberately 400 (A1), but the PLAN has no rule for an empty `week`. The Angular URL layer might emit `week=` for "latest". Decide whether empty `week` means default or 400. Currently it is 400, which is consistent with `type=`.

## Verdict: APPROVED
The objective items (a1–a3) are for the test-author and implementer, not a redo of the contracts. Item a2 is a PLAN edit for the coordinator. The decisions in (b) do not block the red suite, but (b2) should be settled before the API tests are written.

## Next
- The test-author should include the exact `dataAsOf` "Z" golden, the empty and malformed `week` / `type` cases, and the two calendar exception assertions.
- The coordinator should update PLAN §5.2 for the nullable `dataAsOf`.</result>

<result>## Summary
I agree with the coordinator: `IWeekCalendar` should shrink from five members plus a custom exception to three members plus the `WeekWindow` record. It keeps taking the time zone as a `string` on each call. Nothing was edited or committed.

## Changed
None. This is a proposal only.

## Verification
- Read the current contract at `/Users/josemejia/Documents/Software Development/Qualitara/relay-activity-health/.claude/worktrees/agent-aa6eafea36accc597/src/Relay.Core/Calendar/` (`IWeekCalendar.cs`, `WeekCalendar.cs`, `WeekWindow.cs`, `NotAWeekStartException.cs`).
- Searched with `grep` for every use of the calendar across `src`, `tests`, `docs`, `PLAN.md` and `.claude/agents`:
  - `IWeekCalendar` is only used by `ActivityHealthService` (constructor) and the DI registration.
  - `WeekWindow` is used by `IWeeklyGridBuilder` and `IActivityQueries`.
  - `NotAWeekStartException`, `IsWeekStart` and `BaselineWindows` have no callers.
  - No test files exist yet, so nothing is lost by changing this now.
- I did not run a build because nothing changed.

### (1) Proposed contract
```csharp
namespace Relay.Core.Calendar;

public interface IWeekCalendar
{
    WeekWindow Window(DateOnly weekStart, string timeZoneId);

    DateOnly WeekContaining(DateTime instantUtc, string timeZoneId);

    DateOnly LatestCompleteWeek(DateTime dataAnchorUtc, string timeZoneId);
}

public sealed record WeekWindow(DateOnly WeekStart, DateTime StartUtc, DateTime EndUtc);
```
Failure behaviour:
- **Invalid IANA id:** `TimeZoneNotFoundException` from `TimeZoneInfo.FindSystemTimeZoneById` is allowed to propagate. There is no custom type and no fallback to UTC.
- **Non-Monday `weekStart` passed to `Window`:** a plain `ArgumentException`. This is a quick check for programming errors, not for user input.

Checking the coordinator's observations:
- **`IsWeekStart`:** agreed. It is `DayOfWeek == Monday` and has no dependency on time zones, so it doesn't belong on an interface.
- **`BaselineWindows`:** agreed. It is one line of LINQ over `Window` with a single caller, so it removes no duplication.
- **`NotAWeekStartException`:** agreed. The service returns `InvalidWeek(NotAWeekStart)` before it ever calls the calendar. The exception adds a public type and four constructors (needed for analyzer compliance) to guard a path users can't reach.
- **`LatestCompleteWeek`:** I'm challenging the implied option of dropping it. It is `WeekContaining(anchor).AddDays(-7)`, but it is a named domain concept used three ways (default week, upper-bound validation, `earliestWeek` fallback). PLAN §7 lists it as a calendar edge case, and keeping it means BL-41f stays a pure unit test.

On `string timeZoneId` versus resolving `TimeZoneInfo` once:
- **Keep the string.** The account record already carries the id as a string. The runtime caches zone lookups, so there is no performance reason to resolve once. And there is one place where string becomes zone (the calendar), so BL-41g stays a pure calendar test.
- **Passing `TimeZoneInfo` instead** would move id resolution into the service. BL-41g would then need a service test with fakes, and it would add a failure mode that `ActivityHealthResult` has no case for.

### (2) What moves where
- **Monday check:** moves into `ActivityHealthService`: `selectedWeek.DayOfWeek != DayOfWeek.Monday` → `InvalidWeek(NotAWeekStart)`. This covers BL-43.
- **Baseline windows:** built in `ActivityHealthService` as `Enumerable.Range(1, _normalityOptions.BaselineWeeks).Select(weeksBack =&gt; _weekCalendar.Window(selectedWeek.AddDays(-7 * weeksBack), timeZoneId))`, ordered oldest first. Each window is still built boundary by boundary, so baseline weeks that cross a DST change stay correct.
- **`earliestWeek`:** `WeekContaining(firstEventUtc, tz)` in the service, as today.

### (3) Knock-on changes
- **Compile surface:**
  - `IWeekCalendar.cs` and `WeekCalendar.cs` lose two members.
  - `NotAWeekStartException.cs` is deleted.
  - `IWeeklyGridBuilder`, `IActivityQueries`, `ActivityHealthService` (constructor), `SqlActivityQueries` and `CoreServiceCollectionExtensions` are unchanged.
- **`docs/acceptance-criteria.md`:**
  - BL-41a–f: unchanged.
  - BL-41g: make the "Then" column specific: "throws `TimeZoneNotFoundException`".
  - BL-41h: replace with "`Window` for 2026-07-21 (Tue) throws `ArgumentException`". The Monday check itself is tested by BL-43.
  - New BL-46 (suggested): "The service asks for counts for W plus the 8 preceding Mondays. Windows come from the calendar; for America/Chicago with W = 2026-03-16, the baseline includes the 167 h DST week." Without this, nothing pins the baseline window composition once it leaves the calendar.
- **PLAN:** no edit to §6, because §13 is append-only and rules. Add a §13 entry: "`IWeekCalendar` reduced to `Window` / `WeekContaining` / `LatestCompleteWeek`; the week-start check and the baseline window list move to the service; no custom exception". §7's "non-Monday week rejected" is still satisfied, by BL-43 plus the new BL-41h.
- **`.claude/agents/backend.md`:** line 28 is unaffected. `reviewer.md` line 20 still applies.

### (4) Alternatives
- **A. Recommended: three members, string id** (above). This is the smallest surface that keeps every PLAN §7 calendar edge case as a pure unit test.
- **B. Two members: drop `LatestCompleteWeek`.** It's the leanest possible, but the "minus 7" rule moves into the service. BL-41f would have to be rewritten as `WeekContaining` boundary cases (07-27 / 07-20 / 07-27) plus a service test for the subtraction. It saves one line and costs clarity.
- **C. Resolve once with a factory: `IWeekCalendarFactory.ForTimeZone(string)` returning an `IAccountWeekCalendar` with parameterless-zone members.** Call sites get shorter, but it adds two interfaces to remove one argument. That's more engineering, not less. Rejected.

## Deviations
None. The proposal is still within PLAN §6 (behaviour behind an interface, registered in DI) and §13.

## Uncertain
- **Invalid IANA id:** whether the user wants it surfaced through `ActivityHealthResult` rather than an exception. Seed time zones are all valid, so I proposed the exception, which becomes a 500 via `UseExceptionHandler`.
- **Order of `baselineWindows`:** oldest first is my choice. The grid tests should not depend on it, but BL-46 should state it.

## Next
If the user approves option A, the architect applies it:
- edit the two calendar files and delete the exception;
- run `dotnet build` (0 warnings) and `cd web &amp;&amp; npm run build` (no web impact is expected);
- append the §13 entry;
- ask `product` to update BL-41g/h and add BL-46 before `test-author` starts Phase 1.</result>

<result>Findings (efficiency angle):

- `src/Relay.Core/Queries/IActivityQueries.cs:802-810` — three separate activity calls (anchor, first activities, weekly counts), plus `IAccountQueries.FindAsync`. That is 4 round trips per request, and the `ActivityHealthService` will probably await them one after another. The `ListSiteFirstActivitiesAsync` scan is a full-account `MIN(occurred_at)` group-by over history, repeated on every request.
  - Cheaper: the anchor and account lookup don't depend on each other, and the first-activities query doesn't depend on the anchor. Start `FindAsync`, `FindDataAnchorAsync` and `ListSiteFirstActivitiesAsync` together, then run the counts after the anchor resolves.
  - A single `IActivityQueries` method that returns anchor, first activities and counts as one result set is the bigger saving, but it needs the baseline windows up front, which come from the anchor.
  - The contract can stay as it is if the service runs the independent calls concurrently.
  - Caution: EF's `DbContext` is not thread-safe, and both query classes share one scoped context. Parallel calls would need a `DbContext` factory (`AddDbContextFactory`) or separate connections. So this is a contract-level choice.
  - No PLAN-pinned boundary changes, because SQL still only counts.

- `src/Relay.Core/Queries/IActivityQueries.cs:802` and `src/Relay.Core/Queries/IAccountQueries.cs:788` — the anchor (`MAX` over the whole activity table) and the account row are re-queried per request even though they change rarely.
  - Cost: a table-wide aggregate on every dashboard load, and the account list is re-read on every page load.
  - Cheaper: cache the anchor briefly (a decorator, or `IMemoryCache` inside `SqlActivityQueries`). Or derive the anchor from the same statement as the counts (`MAX` over the query's date range), which removes one round trip.
  - Caching the account list is safe. Caching the anchor is only safe if the data is static, which the seed suggests. Check that against PLAN §5.1 before doing it.

- `src/Relay.Core/Queries/IActivityQueries.cs:806-810` and `src/Relay.Core/Normality/IWeeklyGridBuilder.cs:613-623` — `WeeklySiteCount` is passed to `BuildLocationSeries` and again to `BuildAccountSeries`, so the implementation is likely to group and zero-fill the same rows twice.
  - Cost: two passes over roughly 9 weeks × N sites, which is small. The bigger cost is that the account series is derivable from the location series.
  - Cheaper: build `BuildAccountSeries` from `IReadOnlyList&lt;LocationSeries&gt;` by summing eligible sites. That avoids re-deriving eligibility and reuses the first-activity lookup. The catch is that the account total must exclude ineligible site-weeks exactly as PLAN §5.3 says, so this is a Core-internal change, not a PLAN boundary change.

- `src/Relay.Api/Composition/CoreServiceCollectionExtensions.cs:43` — the singleton factory lambda `serviceProvider =&gt; ...GetRequiredService&lt;IOptions&lt;NormalityOptions&gt;&gt;().Value` captures nothing large.
  - Cost: it resolves once, since the singleton is cached. `ValidateOnStart` runs once at startup. This is negligible, so leave it as it is.

- `src/Relay.Api/Composition/CoreServiceCollectionExtensions.cs:50-51` — `AccountService` and `ActivityHealthService` are Scoped, but their only scoped dependencies are the query classes. This is correct, because the queries hold the `DbContext`. Note that the singleton calendar, grid builder, evaluator and ranker stay singleton (good).

- `web/src/app/features/dashboard/dashboard-state.ts:1146-1153` — `accounts` and `report` are separate signals. If they are backed by two independent `rxResource`s or `effect`s, the account list refetches whenever `report` reloads.
  - Cheaper: load the account list once at `DashboardState` construction and never key it to the URL params. Key `report` only to `{accountId, week, eventType}`, and use `rxResource` so a changed param cancels the in-flight request. Add no `effect` that captures the whole state. `reload()` should re-trigger only the report resource.
  - The account name is already returned in the report (`account` in the response), so the list only feeds the selector.

- `web/src/app/app.config.ts:938-939` — `useClass` with `@Injectable()` and no `providedIn` is fine and lazy. No cost.

Everything else, including the API endpoint, the response records, the models and the options validation, is none.</result>
